# Studio competitive-model workspace

This checkout is the isolated starting point for improving UMI's public reference model.

## Bound starting point

- Source repository: https://github.com/Umi-BitSign/umi-reference-model
- Source commit: `abc1a32bf59b234b3f4910bfce3e008ae67fe3fd`
- Local branch: `studio-competitive-training`
- Frozen baseline model identity: `6fe8df59ec11ba89f4dfe0474a673fe757e378cd9184449965861c5a7c59b641`
- Independent local baseline: `artifacts/baseline-6fe8df59/`
- Baseline `MODEL_IDENTITY.json` SHA-256: `50b948db61f8d621af082d6f9bd79c78eb01de7861b1c0807735b2b2e290f4a3`

The artifact directory is an APFS copy-on-write clone. It begins byte-identical to the preserved baseline but is independent of the live miner. The live serving release and its state remain under `/Users/sam/.local/share/umi/`; do not edit or repoint them from this workspace.

## First objective

Produce a reproducible model that beats the frozen baseline on source-disjoint How2Sign and signer-disjoint FLEURS development data and remains compatible with the published UMI bundle runtime. Keep a clean experiment ledger so a result can be reconstructed, licensed, packaged and submitted to the public-model track.

### Current owner direction,2026-10-16

Terra medium0-10 semantic quality is now decisive for training progress and checkpoint selection. WER is a side measurement and must not veto a semantic improvement. This explicitly supersedes historical WER-first acceptance rules for future experiments; preserve their original plans, measurements and decisions as historical evidence. Do not overwrite original baseline values or retrospectively call an unmeasured checkpoint a semantic failure. Numerical/resource qualification, data separation, licenses, serving compatibility and independent confirmation still apply.

`tools/terra_semantic_selection.py` implements a semantic-only research gate: complete calibrated paired scoring, at least5% relative mean-quality gain (with a0.1-point absolute floor), and a positive source-group-bootstrap95% lower bound. Tests explicitly accept better semantics despite worse WER and reject worse semantics despite better WER. This is an intermediate research gate, not automatic serving promotion or proof of dramatic general improvement. The next curriculum pilot should optimize Terra expected error directly; any auxiliary loss must be declared separately. A new recipe cannot silently reuse the old WER-stopping evaluator.

The owner also requests a word/short-phrase-to-complex-phrase curriculum. Use genuinely labeled complete clips, not words cut from a long transcript without video alignment. Retain easier material during later phases. A new isolated-word development set must keep signers/recording groups out of all subsequent local curriculum training; shared public word-class labels across train/development are intentional for known-word recognition, while existing protected sentence references remain excluded. Such an arm must not initialize from our previous locally trained checkpoints that already saw its newly reserved signers. Word learning does not by itself prove continuous-sign translation quality.

Start by reproducing baseline inference and measuring its task-level errors. Then make one measurable change at a time. Favor data quality, split integrity and a strong fingerspelling path over blind hyperparameter sweeps.

The public request includes a fingerspelling/short-utterance/continuous stratum, but the currently pinned native worker passes only video bytes and a deadline to the model runtime. A task-specific fingerspelling route therefore needs a separately verified worker-interface change or an independently tested video-only classifier; changing a model bundle alone will not expose the stratum. Keep that work separate from the active miner.

## Current offline evidence (2026-10-08)

- The one-epoch decoder LoRA run on all 313 FLEURS training clips was rejected. Mean WER on the seven frozen tuning clips rose from 0.9730 for the baseline to 1.0216. The 46 FLEURS confirmation clips remain unused. See `runs/fleurs-full-v1/decision.json`.
- The aligned How2Sign training selection contains 1,024 official training clips (about 1.77 hours). Each selected original-timestamp interval overlaps the publisher's manually realigned interval by at least 80% in both directions. An audit of the official training transcript and frontal-clip archive index found 5,622 clips (about 9.53 hours) meeting the same alignment, duration, transcript-length and file-size filters before source-video caps. A cap of four clips per source video permits at most 3,389 of those clips. The earlier unfiltered scale selection is rejected. See `runs/how2sign-aligned-v1/selection.json` and `runs/how2sign-scale-v1/decision.json`.
- A separate 3,389-clip How2Sign expansion selection is frozen from the same training-only sources. It contains every clip in the 1,024-clip selection plus 2,365 more (5.98 hours total), with at most four clips per source video. The 1,024 original clip receipts were rebound after rehashing their bytes. The stage-one visual-adapter run used the 3,359 clips then receipted, including 2,335 new caches with visual streams. A later publisher-range retry recovered four more selected clips, bringing the current receipt count to 3,363; those four have not been encoded or used for training. The publisher again stopped returning valid byte ranges, leaving 26 selected clips unreceipted. The 1,024 original encoder states were verified and rebound to the expanded selection in `runs/how2sign-expanded-v1/encoder-cache-rebound.jsonl`. The training loader rehashed the caches it used and checked stream shapes and dtypes. The expansion is signer-skewed: signer 8 supplies 2,502 of 3,389 selected clips, so the training plan balances signer exposures. See `runs/how2sign-expanded-v1/selection.json` and `runs/how2sign-expanded-v1/clip-receipts.jsonl`.
- [How2Sign's publisher](https://how2sign.github.io/) says the manually realigned intervals can be cut from the full frontal videos. The local realigned training metadata contains 22,739 intervals totaling 36.31 hours after a 2-12 second duration and three-word minimum filter, before video validation or source caps. The official raw frontal training archive has ten split ZIP parts totaling 290.16 GiB. The first five verified training videos covered signers 1, 2, 5, 9 and 11. Their 65 available realigned intervals were cropped against verified source videos and the publisher's transcript. The 29 signer-5 and signer-2 clips from the first pilots are encoded and frozen in `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/pilot-training-index.jsonl` and `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/signer2-training-index.jsonl`. None has been used for training. See `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/archive-manifest.json` and `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/video-receipts.jsonl`.
- The original raw-video expansion plan selected 53 source videos with 887 metadata-eligible intervals across eight signers. All 53 videos were downloaded and verified against the official split archive. Five publisher intervals in two videos extend past the verified video end; the source-duration-checked revision excludes those five and freezes 882 available intervals (1.35 hours). All 882 have been cropped, receipted and encoded. The aggregate training index is frozen at `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/expansion-training-index-v2.jsonl` after rechecking publisher alignment, source-video hashes, clip receipts and encoder caches. Of those caches, 491 include visual streams and the other 391 now have sidecars. The training loader rehashed all 882 caches and checked stream shapes, dtypes and sidecar provenance after the backfill completed. The later visual-adapter run used this raw expansion data. The compressed source members total 8.49 GiB. Source video IDs are disjoint from the pre-cut training selection and official validation metadata. See `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/expansion-selection.json`, `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/expansion-selection-v2.json` and `runs/how2sign-raw-v1/video-receipts.jsonl`.
- The separate How2Sign validation selection has 58 clips from distinct source videos, with no source-video ID shared with the official training metadata. Its first 16 clips are fixed as tuning; the other 42 are sealed confirmation. The frozen baseline's mean single-reference WER on tuning is 0.8891. This is a development diagnostic, not an official UMI score. See `runs/how2sign-validation-v1/partition.json`.
- The first aligned mixed LoRA pilot trained for one epoch on 168 aligned How2Sign and 313 FLEURS clips. It failed the frozen tuning gate: How2Sign mean WER rose from 0.8891 to 0.9392 on 16 clips, and FLEURS mean WER rose from 0.9730 to 1.1215 on seven clips. Retain the baseline; neither confirmation set was scored. The exact training index was copied into the run ledger before expanding the cache. See `runs/aligned-mixed-pilot-v1/decision.json` and `runs/aligned-mixed-pilot-v1/how2sign-training-index.jsonl`.
- The 1,024 aligned How2Sign encoder states were frozen in selection order and combined with 313 FLEURS training states for a one-epoch cross-attention run. All 1,337 optimizer steps completed and the checkpoint was evaluated on the frozen tuning clips. It failed the two-domain gate: How2Sign mean WER rose from 0.8891 to 0.9163 on 16 clips, while FLEURS rose from 0.9730 to 1.2570 on seven clips. Retain the baseline; no confirmation set was scored and the live miner was unchanged. See `runs/how2sign-aligned-v1/training-index.jsonl`, `runs/full-cross-attention-v1/plan.json`, `checkpoints/full-cross-attention-v1/run.json` and `runs/full-cross-attention-v1/decision.json`.
- A separate one-epoch run updated the complete ByT5 decoder and output head on the same 1,337 frozen training states, leaving the video encoder frozen. All four scheduled checkpoints failed the two-domain tuning gate. The least harmful checkpoint, after 336 examples, raised How2Sign mean WER from 0.8891 to 0.9330 and FLEURS from 0.9730 to 1.0233; later checkpoints were worse. Retain the baseline. The sealed confirmation sets were not scored and the live miner was unchanged. See `runs/full-decoder-v1/plan.json`, `checkpoints/full-decoder-v1/run.json` and `runs/full-decoder-v1/decision.json`.
- Separate visual-stream caches now cover all seven frozen FLEURS and 16 frozen How2Sign tuning clips. Their encoder states are byte-identical to the original caches, and baseline decoding from the visual streams reproduced all 23 original hypotheses and WER values. A zero-initialized adapter for the last four SHuBERT attention layers, visual projection and decoder cross-attention installs on the real baseline with 1,500,684 trainable parameters and leaves its initial encoder output unchanged. One raw-video training clip passed a finite forward/backward/optimizer smoke test before the full run. All 313 FLEURS training-stream caches have byte-identical encoder states to the original frozen caches; `runs/fleurs-dev-v1/training-stream-fidelity.json` records the complete check. The old index uses a different row order and labels some clips with a different decode strategy, but their frozen states match. Sidecars now cover all 1,024 original How2Sign training caches; the training loader rehashed the full source and validated their stream shapes, dtypes and provenance. The loader also checks frame alignment and signer-balanced scheduling before the data can enter a frozen training plan. See `runs/fleurs-dev-v1/tuning-stream-fidelity.json`, `runs/how2sign-validation-v1/stream-fidelity.json` and `runs/visual-lora-smoke-v1/result.json`.
- The source-complete visual-adapter plan is frozen at `runs/visual-lora-stage1-v1/plan.json` (SHA-256 `5a718004a1b244f54f37ffd23440fee34e2fd6e02c182f3952bc27c38e5f70ec`). It covers all 313 FLEURS training streams, 3,359 receipted pre-cut How2Sign streams and 882 raw-video streams, and verifies frozen selections, cache and sidecar hashes, signer coverage and source-video disjointness from validation. An audit of the previous 4,096-example schedule found signer 8 supplied about 42% of How2Sign exposures. The frozen schedule puts signer 8 at about 22%, each other How2Sign signer at about 10-12%, FLEURS at about 17% of all examples and raw How2Sign at about 38%. The stage-one trainer saves recovery states with optimizer and RNG state every 128 examples and marks checkpoints after 512, 1,024, 2,048 and 4,096 examples for tuning. A stopped run can resume from a committed recovery checkpoint into a new run directory without changing the source run. Candidate checkpoints must pass the two-domain WER gate on seven frozen FLEURS and 16 frozen How2Sign visual-stream tuning examples before confirmation can be considered. The live miner remains separate. See `tools/freeze_visual_lora_plan.py`, `tools/train_visual_lora.py` and `tools/decide_visual_lora.py`.
- The first visual-adapter run completed all 4,096 scheduled examples and 1,024 optimizer updates. Its ledger contains 32 committed recovery checkpoints, including the four frozen tuning checkpoints after 512, 1,024, 2,048 and 4,096 examples. All four failed the two-domain tuning gate. At the final checkpoint, FLEURS mean WER improved from 0.9730 to 0.8721, but How2Sign worsened from 0.8891 to 0.9820 and only two of five tuning signers improved. A frozen half-strength interpolation reduced the How2Sign regression to 0.9057 and retained a smaller FLEURS improvement to 0.9435; it also failed the gate. Restoring the baseline visual projection while keeping the trained LoRA weights improved both tuning means, to 0.8726 on How2Sign and 0.9643 on FLEURS, but the gains still failed the gate. All three decisions retain the baseline, neither sealed confirmation set was scored, and no checkpoint was promoted to the live miner. See `checkpoints/visual-lora-stage1-v1/run.json`, `runs/visual-lora-stage1-v1/tuning-decision.json`, `runs/visual-lora-midpoint-v1/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/visual-lora-projection-ablation-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- Restoring the baseline visual projection at earlier stage-one checkpoints gave How2Sign WER 0.8783 after 512 examples, 0.8808 after 1,024 and 0.8746 after 2,048. An exact equal-weight merge of the step-512 and final LoRA deltas improved How2Sign to 0.8600 and FLEURS to 0.9480, with four of five How2Sign signers improving. The equal-domain mean WER change was -0.0271. The merge concatenates equal-rank LoRA factors, so it represents the weighted delta exactly instead of approximating it through factor interpolation. See `tools/ablate_visual_projection.py`, `tools/blend_visual_lora_adapters.py` and `checkpoints/visual-lora-blend-0512-final-w50-v1/adapter.safetensors`.
- A frozen 512-example corrective schedule started from that equal-weight merge and emphasized FLEURS plus the two weakest How2Sign signers while retaining examples from every available training signer. The step-384 checkpoint is the strongest balanced candidate so far: How2Sign WER improved from 0.8891 to 0.8555, FLEURS improved from 0.9730 to 0.9317, and four of five How2Sign tuning signers improved. Its equal-domain mean WER change is -0.0375. Signer 2 worsened from 0.8452 to 0.8770, and the How2Sign mean reduction of 0.0336 misses the frozen 0.05 threshold. Even an oracle that chooses the lowest-WER existing hypothesis per clip from the baseline and corrective steps 128, 256 and 384 reaches only 0.8451, above the required 0.8391. Existing checkpoint selection cannot pass the gate without producing new hypotheses. The formal decision therefore retains the baseline. Confirmation remains sealed and the miner was not changed. See `runs/visual-lora-corrective-v1/plan.json`, `runs/visual-lora-corrective-v1/tuning-decision.json`, `runs/visual-lora-corrective-v1/how2sign-frontier-analysis.json` and `checkpoints/visual-lora-corrective-v1/visual-lora-after-000384.safetensors`.
- Temporal stride 2 produced the new hypotheses the corrective frontier lacked. The fixed step-384 adapter plus every-second-frame preprocessing passed the open-tuning gate and was evaluated once on both previously sealed confirmation sets. On 42 source-video-disjoint How2Sign clips, mean WER improved from 0.9284 to 0.8935 and word-weighted WER from 0.8777 to 0.8609; signers 1, 3 and 8 improved, while the three signer-5 clips worsened. On 46 FLEURS-ASL clips from 23 held-out documents, mean WER improved from 1.0497 to 0.9578 and word-weighted WER from 1.0506 to 0.9630. The equal-domain mean change was -0.0634, passing the confirmation gate. No other confirmation checkpoint, stride or decoder setting was evaluated. The adapter has been merged into a conventional checkpoint at `checkpoints/c5-corrective-stride2-confirmed`; all 88 merged-checkpoint hypotheses exactly reproduce the confirmed adapter-form outputs. Its model SHA-256 is `419315ded8438bb59fe1b1a7e3c774012e098067db21335621789b56e9f98210`. A 452-file, 2.93 GB standalone bundle is at `artifacts/c5-corrective-stride2-confirmed`; its complete `SHA256SUMS` manifest passes verification and its runtime loads the merged assets successfully. The runtime runs MediaPipe on every source frame, then retains frames 0, 2, 4 and so on before crop extraction, DINO, pose features and C5. This matches the confirmed cached-stream experiment while avoiding DINO work on the skipped frames. The bundle has not been installed into the miner. See `runs/temporal-stride-v1/confirmation-decision.json`, `runs/temporal-stride-v1/merged-export-equivalence.json`, `tools/c5_stride2_runtime.py` and `tools/build_c5_stride2_bundle.py`.
- A training-only decode-length screen tested beam penalties 1.1, 1.2 and 1.3 on 28 How2Sign and four FLEURS clips with the step-384 adapter. Penalty 1.1 increased the mean How2Sign hypothesis length from 9.46 to 9.75 words and reduced mean WER by 0.0052, but the gain was confined to one of five primary signers. Penalties 1.2 and 1.3 worsened both domains. No setting passed the frozen screen, so none advanced to tuning. See `runs/visual-lora-corrective-v1/decode-length-training-probe.json`.
- A second plan, `runs/visual-lora-projection-frozen-v1/plan.json` (SHA-256 `7422305f04a1ed31040eb7a354171a64516f2a772a132f6ab0e7224386620319`), started from the projection ablation, reused the same 4,096-example public-data schedule, and trained only the SHuBERT and decoder LoRA tensors at a lower learning rate. All 4,096 examples and 1,024 updates completed. The three direct visual-projection tensors were excluded from the optimizer and remained byte-identical to the baseline at all four tuning checkpoints. None passed the frozen gate. The 1,024-example checkpoint gave the best How2Sign mean WER in this run, 0.8810 versus 0.8891 for the baseline, but FLEURS worsened to 1.0078 versus 0.9730 and only two of five How2Sign signers improved. The final checkpoint worsened both domains, to 0.9374 on How2Sign and 1.2050 on FLEURS. Retain the baseline; neither sealed confirmation set was scored and no checkpoint was promoted. The low-priority trainer checked the live miner around each example and optimizer step. See `checkpoints/visual-lora-projection-frozen-v1/run.json` and `runs/visual-lora-projection-frozen-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- A metadata-only audit of unused realigned How2Sign raw training intervals found 714 videos with at least four eligible non-overlapping clips after excluding validation and the pre-cut training source videos. Their 7,007 intervals total 11.65 hours on paper, before checking archive membership, source bytes or actual video duration. Signers 5 and 8 account for 672 of those 714 videos and 11.11 of the 11.65 hours; signer 4 has one additional video, and signers 2 and 9 have three each. The [publisher's pre-cut archive](https://how2sign.github.io/#download) briefly answered exact byte ranges and matched the frozen central-directory fingerprint, but the current 3,389-clip selection already includes every clip that passes its four-per-video cap and alignment filter. Its range response failed again after four more clips were fetched. The separate raw-video archive also failed its exact-range check. More clips from these same sources would add little signer diversity, so a larger training run needs another source or a different sampling design. See `runs/how2sign-raw-capacity-v1/audit.json` and `tools/audit_how2sign_raw_capacity.py`.
- Relaxing only the pre-cut four-per-source-video cap could add at most 2,275 metadata-eligible intervals beyond the 3,389 already selected. Of these, 1,632 are signer 8; the other seven signers together add at most 643 intervals or 0.96 hours. The audit checks transcript duration, words and realigned overlap, but not archive membership or member size, so these are upper bounds. The additional intervals reuse source videos already represented in training. See `runs/how2sign-precut-capacity-v1/audit.json`.
- [Microsoft's ASL STEM Wiki](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/project/asl-stem-wiki/dataset-description/) provides sentence-aligned STEM signing from 37 consented interpreters. Under the reported public-model-track counsel advice, this noncommercial research source is eligible for training while its videos and modified data remain undistributed. The official 201.2 GB ZIP supports exact HTTP ranges, so the full archive is not needed. Its 64,266-member video inventory, metadata and license are pinned. An audit found 15,775 clips passing the 2-12 second, 3-30 word, archive-integrity and protected-reference filters: 39.58 hours across 36 usable interpreters and 252 articles, with no exact reference overlap against the protected How2Sign and FLEURS evaluation sets. A deterministic 1,024-clip training selection spans all 36 usable interpreters, 169 articles and 916 normalized references. It totals 2.65 hours and 1.67 GB compressed. All 1,024 selected videos are downloaded and verified by archive metadata, CRC, file hash and media probe. A pinned Linux CPU MediaPipe image has also produced and receipted 1,024 aspect-preserving 25 fps clips and landmark sidecars covering 237,270 frames; exact downstream decode fidelity was established before the full run. See `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/source-manifest.json`, `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/metadata-audit.json`, `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/training-selection.json`, `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/video-receipts.jsonl` and `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/landmark-receipts.jsonl`.
- Stride-2 C5 stream caches are complete for all 1,024 selected ASL STEM Wiki clips. They represent 237,270 source frames as 118,877 cached frames under `data/asl-stem-wiki/stream-cache-stride2`; `runs/asl-stem-wiki-v1/stream-cache-stride2.jsonl` binds each cache to its source and preprocessing receipts. A separate, CPU-only continuation started from the confirmed corrective adapter and mixed 256 STEM, 192 How2Sign and 64 FLEURS exposures. Its final step improved open-tuning mean WER from 0.8341 to 0.8244 on How2Sign and from 0.8806 to 0.8800 on FLEURS, but only two of five How2Sign signers improved. It failed the predeclared three-signer gate, so the branch is closed without confirmation reuse or promotion. The confirmed stride-2 candidate remains selected. See `runs/c5-stem-continuation-v1/plan.json` and `runs/c5-stem-continuation-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- Two controls tested whether that STEM continuation could retain broader signer behavior. A frozen interpolation grid put 25%, 50% and 75% weight on the final STEM adapter. None passed: the 25% point worsened How2Sign by 0.0060, while the heavier points improved only signers 2 and 5 and slightly worsened FLEURS. A matched continuation then replayed the same 512 examples in the same order while keeping the three direct visual-projection tensors byte-identical to the confirmed adapter. From step 256 onward it changed How2Sign mean WER from 0.8341 to 0.8312, left every FLEURS hypothesis unchanged at 0.8806 and improved only signer 2. Direct-projection drift caused the additional signer regressions in the first continuation, but freezing it did not produce a broad gain. Both controls are closed without confirmation reuse or promotion. See `runs/c5-stem-interpolation-v1/decision.json` and `runs/c5-stem-projection-frozen-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- A disjoint supplemental ASL STEM Wiki tranche adds 1,024 clips, 2.62 hours, 30 interpreters, 178 articles and 951 normalized references. Combined with the first tranche, the prepared corpus has 2,048 clips, 5.27 hours, 201 articles and 1,831 normalized references. Every supplemental source video, 25 fps clip, landmark sidecar and stride-2 C5 stream cache is receipted. The supplemental caches represent 234,738 source frames as 117,610 stride-2 frames. See `runs/asl-stem-wiki-scale-v1/supplemental-selection-1024.json`, `runs/asl-stem-wiki-scale-v1/video-receipts.jsonl`, `runs/asl-stem-wiki-scale-v1/landmark-receipts.jsonl` and `runs/asl-stem-wiki-scale-v1/stream-cache-stride2.jsonl`.
- A projection-frozen scale run consumed all 2,048 STEM clips once, plus 752 signer-balanced How2Sign examples and 312 FLEURS examples. The final 3,112-example checkpoint reduced open-tuning How2Sign mean WER from 0.8341 to 0.8129 and left every FLEURS hypothesis unchanged at 0.8806. Signers 2 and 5 improved, signer 1 regressed, signer 8 slipped and signer 3 was unchanged. This failed the predeclared requirement that at least three of five How2Sign signers improve. Exact 25%, 50% and 75% LoRA-delta blends also failed that gate; the 75% blend had the best How2Sign change at -0.0229 with FLEURS unchanged, but still improved only signers 2 and 5. The confirmed stride-2 candidate remains selected, and neither consumed confirmation set was reused. See `runs/c5-stem-scale-v1/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-stem-scale-interpolation-v1/decision.json`.
- Three projection-frozen signer-recovery probes replayed 640 How2Sign examples, balanced at 80 per training signer, plus 128 FLEURS examples from the scaled STEM endpoint. Learning rates `1e-6`, `2e-6` and `4e-6` were tested against the same frozen schedule. The `1e-6` run changed no tuning hypotheses. The two stronger runs improved FLEURS from 0.8806 to 0.8735 while preserving the scaled endpoint's 0.8129 How2Sign mean, but every How2Sign hypothesis stayed unchanged. Signers 2 and 5 remained the only improved signers, so all three probes failed the signer gate. Further learning-rate escalation on this schedule is closed. See `runs/c5-signer-recovery-v1/tuning-decision.json`, `runs/c5-signer-recovery-v2/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-signer-recovery-v3/tuning-decision.json`.
- A signer-3-focused recovery replay also failed to broaden the scaled endpoint. It used 192 signer-3 examples, 64 from each other How2Sign training signer and 128 FLEURS examples. Every tuning hypothesis remained unchanged at 0.8129 How2Sign and 0.8735 FLEURS, leaving signers 2 and 5 as the only improved signers. Expanding visual LoRA from the last four SHuBERT layers to all 12 layers did not help either. The new layers began as an exact function-preserving expansion, but a full 3,112-example replay never passed the signer gate. Its first checkpoint retained 0.8129 How2Sign and 0.8735 FLEURS; later checkpoints regressed to 0.8173 How2Sign and as high as 0.8803 FLEURS. Both branches are closed without confirmation reuse or promotion. See `runs/c5-signer3-recovery-v1/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-expanded-visual-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- The OpenASL diversity pilot pinned 98,420 metadata rows from 2,043 source videos. A conservative training-only filter retains 61,018 clips and 107.92 hours. Because the official train, validation and test splits share source-video IDs, local candidates exclude every video appearing in official validation or test. All 60 independently probed pilot sources were downloaded without a terminal failure. The frozen selection has 120 clips, no more than two per source, and 0.217 hours of signing. Full-frame and annotated-signer-crop variants each produced 120 aspect-preserving 25 fps clips, MediaPipe sidecars covering 19,486 source frames, and C5 caches covering 9,771 stride-2 frames. Metal extraction used a 50% duty cycle. See `runs/openasl-v1/metadata-audit.json`, `runs/openasl-v1/pilot-selection.json`, `runs/openasl-v1/stream-cache-stride2.jsonl` and `runs/openasl-bbox-v1/stream-cache-stride2.jsonl`.
- Availability probing is now complete for all 256 frozen OpenASL candidate sources: 240 remain reachable and 16 are unavailable. Excluding the 60 already prepared sources freezes a disjoint scale tranche of 180 new source videos and 360 unique clips totaling 0.671 hours. Downloading those complete videos would require approximately 18.32 GiB, whereas bounded time-range retrieval is estimated at 0.51 GiB before container overhead and keyframe margins. A clip-only fetcher preserves exact source URLs and timestamps, hashes every output, retains aspect ratio, fetches serially and uses the bounded clip-only operational storage policy described below. Its downstream path is also wired: each fetched range is normalized aspect-preservingly to 25 fps, processed by the pinned Linux CPU MediaPipe image, and accepted by the existing stride-2 C5 cache tool, whose MPS mode retains the mandatory 50% duty cycle. Before acquisition, a source-level partition was frozen: 240 clips from 120 sources are training-only, one clip from each of 60 other sources is sealed for a single post-selection confirmation, and the second clip from each confirmation source remains unused reserve. The training protocol is also frozen before media acquisition: a 768-example projection-frozen C5 replay uses all 240 training clips, 256 signer-balanced How2Sign clips, 136 FLEURS clips and 136 clips balanced across both ASL-STEM tranches. Its learning rate is `4e-6`, checkpoints are fixed at 128, 256, 512 and 768 examples, and it must improve both open domains, their equal-domain mean by at least 0.01, and at least three How2Sign signers before the sealed 60-source confirmation may be opened. A cache binder rejects any confirmation or reserve example in training. Focused tests cover selection, fetch commands, per-example provenance, source-disjoint partitioning, protocol composition, cache binding and pilot/scale cache dispatch. On 2026-10-12, all 360 source ranges were downloaded and hash-verified using 296.3 MiB; a timestamp-normalization smoke test and the first pinned MediaPipe extraction passed. All 360 normalized clips and landmark files are now verified, covering 60,390 frames. Their complete C5 caches contain 30,280 stride-2 frames, with index SHA-256 `90554e5007275dbd6a4f37059875c15dcf592a0f252b18362dab99204c75dd06`. Source clips, derivatives, landmarks and caches together use 2.25 GiB. The clip-only path now uses an 80 GiB physical free-space floor and a separate 4 GiB data budget; full-video acquisition guards and all scientific selections remain unchanged. See `runs/openasl-scale-v1/selection.json`, `runs/openasl-scale-v1/partition.json`, `runs/c5-openasl-scale-v1/protocol.json`, `tools/freeze_openasl_scale_selection.py`, `tools/fetch_openasl_scale_clips.py` and `tools/prepare_openasl_scale_landmarks.py`.
- Three projection-frozen OpenASL replays started from the confirmed adapter and used the same 512-example schedule: 120 OpenASL, 128 ASL STEM, 192 signer-balanced How2Sign and 72 FLEURS exposures. At learning rate `2e-6`, the best checkpoint improved How2Sign from 0.8341 to 0.8297 and left FLEURS at 0.8806, but only signer 2 improved and signer 1 regressed. Raising the rate to `4e-6` reached 0.8208 How2Sign at step 256 with FLEURS unchanged, but again only signer 2 improved. Its step-512 checkpoint reached 0.8219 with signers 2 and 5 improved, signer 1 worse, and signers 3 and 8 unchanged. A 20-clip OpenASL training-fit sample moved from mean WER 0.6029 to 0.6103, with only four changed hypotheses, one improvement and one regression. The update did not learn the sampled OpenASL captions.
- The annotated signer crop was tested because several OpenASL sources are split-screen broadcasts. It preserves the rectangular metadata region and pads only when a box extends outside the source frame; it does not squash or force a square. Before training, the same 20-clip sample improved from 0.6029 full-frame WER to 0.5829 cropped WER, with seven clips better, eight worse and five tied. Mean OpenASL training loss was 1.2821 for the matched full-frame run and 1.2866 for the crop, so signer isolation did not make the frozen C5 objective easier. Its matched `4e-6` replay converged to the same step-512 open-tuning result as the full-frame run: 0.8219 How2Sign, 0.8806 FLEURS, two signers improved and one regressed. All three OpenASL branches fail the three-signer gate and are closed without confirmation reuse or promotion. The confirmed stride-2 candidate remains selected. See `runs/c5-openasl-pilot-v1/tuning-decision.json`, `runs/c5-openasl-pilot-v2/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-openasl-bbox-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- A baseline video-conditioning probe used five training clips, one each from How2Sign signers 1, 2, 3, 5 and 8. Swapping each clip's cached visual encoder state with the next signer's changed all five decoded hypotheses; mean WER against the clips' own references rose from 0.7306 to 0.9913. This small training-only diagnostic shows the frozen decoder responds to its video representation on these examples. It does not establish generalization or a candidate-model gain. See `runs/visual-conditioning-probe-v1/result.json`.
- A separate training-fit diagnostic decoded 32 deterministic examples that the first visual-adapter run actually saw: 28 How2Sign examples balanced across four training sources and available signers, plus four FLEURS signer-3 examples. On the 28 How2Sign clips, baseline mean WER was 0.8953 and the projection-restored adapter's was 0.8585. For comparison, their frozen How2Sign tuning means were 0.8891 and 0.8726. The training gain was concentrated in eight raw-video clips (mean WER 0.9621 to 0.8490); the ten original pre-cut clips worsened slightly (0.8797 to 0.8897). On the four FLEURS training clips, means were 0.7529 and 0.7543, versus 0.9730 and 0.9643 on the separate signer-4 tuning clips. This small, nonrandom sample does not show a large How2Sign train/tuning gap for the baseline; its selected references are also shorter than tuning references. The distributed baseline lacks an exact training-example inventory, so this cannot rule out overfitting to other original training data. FLEURS signer and clip differences prevent a causal attribution there. No confirmation clip was scored and no model was promoted. See `runs/visual-training-fit-probe-v1/result.json`.
- The [publisher's training state for checkpoint 11625](https://huggingface.co/ShesterG/SHuBERT/blob/main/models/checkpoint-11625/trainer_state.json) supports a narrower overfitting concern. Between steps 5,425 and 11,625, logged training loss fell from 2.0679 to 1.9975 while evaluation loss rose from its minimum of 2.1011 to 2.1421. Over the same interval, evaluation BLEU-4 improved from 27.79 to 29.97 and WER fell from 58.67 to 57.25; the log identifies step 11,625 as the best checkpoint by BLEU-4. The loss divergence is compatible with overfitting or worsening calibration under the training objective, but the task metrics do not show a simple late-checkpoint collapse. The exact training-example inventory and evaluation split details are unavailable, so this log cannot establish whether the distributed model memorizes particular clips or generalizes to this workspace's source-disjoint, signer-disjoint tests. A matched train-versus-held-out evaluation, preferably across multiple publisher checkpoints, would be needed to isolate that question.
- A training-only nearest-caption probe tested the frozen encoder as a retrieval index over all 4,241 cached How2Sign training clips. Each of the 28 training-fit examples queried the cosine-nearest caption after excluding every clip from its own source video. Mean WER was 1.6793 for retrieval versus 0.8953 for baseline decoding; retrieval was better on two clips, worse on 25 and tied on one. This rules out that specific mean-pool, top-one retrieval shortcut. No tuning or confirmation example was used, no model was trained or promoted, and the live miner remained on its original revision. See `runs/visual-retrieval-probe-v1/result.json`.
- A training-only beam-length probe decoded the same 32 training-fit examples at fixed length penalties 0.7, 1.0, 1.3 and 1.6. The 1.0 run exactly reproduced all 32 baseline hypotheses. The best tested alternative, 0.7, changed mean WER by -0.0058 on 28 How2Sign clips and -0.0179 on four FLEURS clips, with only two of five primary How2Sign signers improving. It missed the predeclared gate of -0.05 How2Sign mean WER and at least three improved signers. Penalties 1.3 and 1.6 worsened both domains. No setting advanced to frozen tuning, no confirmation example was scored, and the live miner retained its original revision. See `runs/decode-length-probe-v1/result.json`.
- The [published SHuBERT ablation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1397/) reports higher How2Sign BLEU when it fine-tunes SHuBERT with the translator than when it keeps SHuBERT frozen (7.5 versus 4.7 in that experiment). Those scores do not predict this model's performance. The source-complete visual-adapter run and projection ablation above are this project's direct evidence.
- The pre-cut confirmation set has no signer-2 examples. A separate confirmation supplement is frozen from 17 other official validation videos, with two manually realigned clips per video and four videos each for signers 1, 2, 3 and 5. The selection excludes training and pre-cut validation source-video IDs. Its 2.45 GB of source videos have not been downloaded or scored. See `runs/how2sign-full-video-confirmation-v1/selection.json`.
- A public FSboard fingerspelling pilot favored an offline Core ML specialist over the frozen translation baseline (mean CER 0.145 versus 0.650 on 30 matched clips). The [checkpoint card](https://huggingface.co/Skunk0/openglasses-fingerspelling-ctc) declares Apache-2.0 for its weights and conversion and names the Google ASL Fingerspelling corpus as CC BY 4.0 training data. The [competition data page](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/asl-fingerspelling/data) lists CC BY 4.0 but requires sign-in and agreement to competition rules for access. The original [second-place solution repository](https://github.com/hoyso48/Google---American-Sign-Language-Fingerspelling-Recognition-2nd-place-solution) has model weights and TFLite files, which may address portability, but its root has no license file; do not assume that its code or files share the Core ML checkpoint's declared Apache-2.0 terms. Source-rule compliance, a portable verified bundle and a task-aware worker route remain unresolved. No specialist or translation adapter has been promoted to the miner. See `runs/fsboard-validation-pilot-v1/decision.json`.
- A pinned 11,964,080-byte CTC-greedy TFLite file from that second-place repository has an isolated LiteRT runtime under `artifacts/`. Its SHA-256 is `dd299ab7bbde6be5cc482fc991d7cbd9d7466395781a4e9b029f4e40bcdbd4b4`. The published signature accepts variable-length `float32` frames with 1,629 columns. The publisher's [inference notebook](https://github.com/hoyso48/Google---American-Sign-Language-Fingerspelling-Recognition-2nd-place-solution/blob/main/ASLFR_2nd_place_Hoyeol_Sohn_inference.ipynb) declares columns grouped by body part, but its graph reshapes global x/y/z blocks; the [training notebook](https://github.com/hoyso48/Google---American-Sign-Language-Fingerspelling-Recognition-2nd-place-solution/blob/main/ASLFR_2nd_place_Hoyeol_Sohn_training.ipynb) reads the latter layout. On one public validation clip, the declared-column mapping produced `+1111`, while the training-tensor mapping produced `lnmigan`, matching the Core ML checkpoint. `tools/evaluate_fsboard_tflite.py` records the chosen layout and checks live miner health before loading and around every clip. The training-tensor layout was scored on two disjoint 30-clip public-validation samples: mean single-reference UMI-normalized CER was 0.150 and 0.188, versus 0.145 and 0.200 for Core ML. Across all 60 clips, the means were 0.169 and 0.173, with 38 identical transcriptions. The offline input preflight and seven TFLite/parity unit tests passed. See `runs/fsboard-tflite-parity-v1/summary.json`. The same signers appear in both samples, these are not official multi-reference UMI scores, and checkpoint redistribution rights and native worker task routing remain unresolved. No candidate was promoted to the miner.
- Correction to the FSboard training evidence below: the ten downloaded `daun_v3` Arrow landmark shards have corrupt coordinate arrays. In a deterministic 32-row-per-shard sample, every observed first face frame (319/319), left-hand frame (185/185), right-hand frame (287/287), and pose frame (320/320) had `y[i] == x[i+1]` and `z[i] == y[i+1]` across its coordinate arrays. The arrays are shifted views of interleaved xyz values, leaving roughly two-thirds of the landmarks unrecoverable. The earlier shard audits checked split identity, widths and frame counts, but missed this defect. All three ByT5 runs below trained on these malformed landmarks. Their CER results document the failure of that input pipeline; they cannot support conclusions about ByT5, its hyperparameters or how much clean FSboard data it needs. The separate TFLite and Core ML diagnostics used landmarks extracted from public validation videos, so this Arrow finding does not invalidate those measurements. The revised source audit rejects shifted arrays, and the training loader refuses the old, unverified audits by default. Do not train from these Arrow shards or acquire more of them for training. See `runs/fsboard-coordinate-integrity-v1/result.json` and `tools/audit_fsboard_coordinate_integrity.py`.
- The publisher's [FSboard baseline paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2407.15806) reports 11.1% CER from fine-tuning pretrained ByT5-Small on landmark inputs, versus 33.8% when training the same architecture from scratch; those are publisher test results, not this workspace's scores. The local `daun_v3` training metadata contains 70,202 nonsensitive fingerspelling clips totaling 142.41 hours from 94 signers. Its 15 validation signers and exact phrases are disjoint. The publisher file listing freezes 100 training landmark shards totaling 57.28 GiB in `runs/fsboard-train-landmark-manifest-v1/manifest.json`; no video download is needed for this training source. One published training landmark shard was acquired: 739 SequenceExamples, 736 matched to nonsensitive training rows across 90 signers, and three sensitive rows excluded. Of the 736 safe rows, 681 have a detected hand in at least half their frames. The audit also checked x/y/z frame counts and landmark widths. `runs/fsboard-train-shard00-v3/training-index.jsonl` freezes row offsets and references against the publisher shard and metadata hashes. `tools/fetch_fsboard_train_shard.py` can fetch one more named landmark shard at a time and refuses to train from it; each new shard still needs an independent audit and frozen row index. See `runs/fsboard-train-shard00-v3/audit.json` and `tools/audit_fsboard_train_arrow.py`.
- The [Apache-2.0 ByT5-Small checkpoint](https://huggingface.co/google/byt5-small) is pinned separately from the unlicensed TFLite artifact. An isolated CPU environment loaded the audited training shard, projected its 543 normalized landmarks into the pretrained ByT5 encoder, and completed one optimizer step on a nonsensitive training row. Loss was finite at 6.3076; both the projection and shared ByT5 weights changed. This is only a pipeline smoke test: no candidate checkpoint was saved, no validation was run, and no quality gain is claimed. The live miner was unchanged and passed health checks before and after the step. See `runs/fsboard-byt5-smoke-v1/result.json`, `tools/fsboard_byt5_model.py` and `tools/train_fsboard_byt5_smoke.py`.
- The first ByT5 shard run is a feasibility experiment, not a reproduction of the FSboard paper. It uses 681 examples from one of 100 training landmark shards, 543 points per frame, 15 Hz sampling, per-axis standardization, and a 1e-4 learning rate. The [FSboard paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2407.15806) instead reports 85 selected points, usually 30 Hz, a 0.001 Adafactor base learning rate, batch size 64, and much longer training. The [YouTube-ASL preprocessing description](https://arxiv.org/html/2306.15162) identifies those 85 points as 42 hand points, six pose points, and 37 face points, including iris indices 468 and 473. Our existing 543-point validation caches omit those two iris points. `tools/fsboard_full_landmarks.py` can retain all 478 publisher face points from an audited training shard, and `tools/fsboard_paper_like_features.py` selects either the available 83 points or the full 85 with an explicitly defined unit-box transform and missing-point sentinel. The validation extractor now accepts `--face-points 478` for new, separately receipted 553-point caches; no full-face validation clip has been extracted yet. The full-face loader passed on a real publisher row, but neither transform is used by the running candidate or has a quality result. A closer reproduction needs the full-face validation extraction, more training shards, and a separate pinned evaluation before its reported test CER can be used as a useful target.
- The 681-step ByT5 shard run completed in 332 seconds. Mean training loss fell from 5.016 on its first 64 examples to 2.553 on its last 64. On 60 public, signer-disjoint validation clips, its mean single-reference CER was 1.182 and it transcribed none exactly. The pinned TFLite reference scored 0.169 CER and won all 60 paired comparisons. Outputs frequently repeated digits and punctuation. This run does not establish whether the failure comes from limited data, the preprocessing and training recipe, or both. Its checkpoint is retained only as an experiment artifact and is not eligible for miner promotion. The live miner revision remained unchanged during training and evaluation. See `runs/fsboard-byt5-shard00-v1/result.json`, `runs/fsboard-byt5-shard00-v1/validation-summary.json` and `runs/fsboard-byt5-shard00-v1/paired-comparison.json`.
- Ten published FSboard train landmark shards have now been downloaded and audited. They contain 7,095 nonsensitive examples, of which 6,567 meet the hand-coverage and 256-sampled-frame limits, across 92 signers. Shard 4 contains one valid 788-frame clip, so the source audit now accepts up to 4,096 frames while the trainer retains its separate 512-source-frame limit. A low-priority ByT5 run on all 6,567 eligible examples completed in 3,177 seconds with six recovery checkpoints and no reported miner revision change. Training loss averaged 4.847 over its first 64 examples and 2.187 over its last 64. Its validation CER was 1.027, with zero exact transcriptions; TFLite still won all 60 paired clips at 0.169. The ByT5 model returned one of two near-identical strings on 59 of 60 clips. A deterministic training-only probe sampled four seen clips per shard: mean CER was 0.981 with only three distinct outputs across 40 clips. This checkpoint has not learned reliable transcription even on the probed training examples, so further scaling of this recipe is deferred. See `runs/fsboard-byt5-ten-shard-v1/result.json`, `runs/fsboard-byt5-ten-shard-v1/validation-summary.json` and `runs/fsboard-byt5-ten-shard-v1/training-fit-probe.json`.
- A separate compact-landmark pilot selected the available 83 face, hand and pose points from the same first shard, applied unit-box normalization with a missing-point sentinel, and raised the Adafactor learning rate to 0.001. After 681 steps, its public-validation CER was 4.004 and its 60 outputs collapsed to two repeated strings. This pilot changed both representation and learning rate, so it does not isolate either factor. It is rejected. An attempted full-face validation extraction failed before processing any clip because the local MediaPipe Holistic runtime could not initialize its macOS graphics service; no full-face validation result exists. The available 543-point caches still omit the two iris points needed for the published 85-point selection. See `runs/fsboard-byt5-compact-shard00-v1/result.json` and `runs/fsboard-byt5-compact-shard00-v1/validation-summary.json`.
- A separate [FLEURS-ASL fingerspelling study](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.19/) found that using ByT5 instead of subword-tokenized T5 improved fingerspelled-span CER from 76.6% to 41.6% in its translation setup, while adding FSboard to that setup's translation training mixture changed CER from 41.6% to 42.1% and BLEURT from 45.4 to 45.1. These are the study's scores, not this model's. They argue for testing an isolated specialist and a task route before assuming that mixing FSboard into the current translator will improve continuous translation.
- The fresh raw-frame inventory contains 2,219 training-only examples: 1,024 aligned How2Sign clips, 882 source-video-derived How2Sign clips and 313 FLEURS-ASL clips. It spans 1,062 recording or document groups. The frozen JSONL SHA-256 is `0902fda43f796059b1799bcdb97d46ff3bfa1ef8703a8d0f5160db258db5a60e`. It contains source-video paths and hashes, references, groups and signers, but no C5 checkpoint fields or encoder caches. The external How2Sign tuning clips remain source-video disjoint, and the frozen FLEURS tuning and confirmation clips are not in this inventory. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v1/training-inventory.jsonl` and `tools/freeze_fresh_lowres_inventory.py`.
- Fresh low-resolution pilots rejected the byte-level ByT5 decoder for this continuous-translation inventory. An ImageNet MobileNetV3-Small visual path still generated spaces after 512 alignment examples, even though swapping videos changed teacher-forced logits. Replacing MobileNet with frozen Kinetics-400 S3D features did not change the space-loop failure after 128 examples. These are training-fit failures, not held-out comparisons, and neither checkpoint is eligible for promotion.
- A pinned Apache-2.0 T5-Small artifact at upstream revision `df1b051c49625cf57a3d0d8d3863ed4d13564fe4` replaced byte tokens with subwords for the next fresh pilot. Its local `model.safetensors` SHA-256 is `bd944e5f1b3ad9b70dd9d00010a517059e19265671076b8b0a4a58d9491842bc`. Discarding the unused text encoder leaves 45,250,080 parameters, with 8,989,184 trainable during alignment. After 512 alignment examples, four seen clips had mean WER 0.972 and mostly empty outputs. Updating the retained decoder through all 2,219 unique clips produced fluent but repetitive hallucinations; eight seen clips had mean WER 2.329. This branch fails the training-fit gate, so no held-out tuning or miner promotion was attempted. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-ss192-step512-training-fit.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-ss192-decoder-epoch1-training-fit.json`.
- An auxiliary CTC experiment scores each temporal video state against the normalized, tied T5 token embeddings. Every frozen training target fits within its 12.5-fps temporal sequence. A maintenance-authorized retry completed 512 alignment steps with recovery checkpoints. Mean CTC loss fell from 41.433 over the first eight steps of the branch to 14.686 over global steps 385-512. Its direct CTC readout remained blank on the eight-example probe, but swapping videos increased CTC loss by 2.641 on average over seven feasible pairs. A decoder continuation through global step 768 remained repetitive and had seen-set mean WER 1.583. CTC therefore detects some video-target correspondence but has not produced useful token decoding. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-ctc03-ss192-step512-training-fit.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-ctc03-ss192-decoder-step768-training-fit.json`.
- A 16-example repeated-exposure diagnostic tests whether the fresh T5-Small path can fit before any full-inventory run. Twenty epochs with CTC weight 0.3 and semantic weight 10 reduced semantic contrastive loss from 2.213 in epoch 1 to 1.390 in epoch 20, but generation still looped. A further 20 decoder epochs at learning rate 0.0001, CTC weight 0.1 and semantic weight 1 reduced mean cross-entropy from 6.935 to 1.693 and semantic loss from 1.574 to 0.399. Its eight-clip probe still had mean seen-set WER 1.308. A final 20 epochs using generation loss alone reduced mean cross-entropy to 0.508. Six of eight probe references then decoded exactly, one had WER 0.188, and one retained a repetition failure; mean seen-set WER was 0.172 with eight distinct hypotheses. The architecture therefore passes the small-corpus memorization gate and can learn clip-specific content, but this diagnostic says nothing about held-out quality. Its weights are not a scale-training initializer and are not eligible for miner promotion. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-overfit16-decoder-v2-training-fit.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-overfit16-ce-v3-training-fit.json`.
- The fresh-model tuning inventory freezes only the already-open development material: 16 source-video-disjoint How2Sign clips and seven signer-4 FLEURS clips from three documents. Its SHA-256 is `9a2cbe1e8bfa0e630829d04f6684e58592b5b561644b720b0bbf3b2a12b942d3`. The freezer checks source-group disjointness from all 2,219 training rows and binds the exact 25-fps clip hashes. Confirmation remains sealed. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v1/tuning-inventory-v2.jsonl`, `tools/freeze_fresh_lowres_tuning.py` and `tools/evaluate_fresh_lowres_tuning.py`.
- The first scale checkpoint trained once over all 2,219 fresh-inventory clips at short-side 192. Mean cross-entropy fell from 7.178 over the first 64 examples to 4.053 over the last 64, and mean CTC loss fell from 32.757 to 12.449. Semantic loss only moved from 2.258 to 2.007. On the frozen open tuning inventory, its How2Sign mean WER was 3.954 and its FLEURS mean WER was 1.201, with zero exact outputs and repetition collapse in both domains. This one-pass checkpoint is rejected for promotion; a two-pass continuation tests whether the repeated exposure required by the memorization diagnostic transfers to held-out clips. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-full-v1-epoch1-tuning.json`.
- After two additional mixed-objective passes, mean cross-entropy was 3.816, CTC loss 12.350 and semantic loss 1.933 in the last pass. How2Sign tuning WER improved to 2.260, but FLEURS worsened to 2.492; both still had zero exact outputs and repetition collapse. Eight sampled seen clips also remained poor at mean WER 1.798, with only a 0.683 mean absolute teacher-forced logit change under video swaps. Two generation-only passes lowered mean cross-entropy to 3.302, but the seen probe worsened to WER 2.378. How2Sign tuning moved only to 2.174 and FLEURS deteriorated to 3.648, again with no exact outputs. The direct visual-to-decoder formulation is rejected; further epochs are not justified. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-full-v1-epoch3-tuning.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-full-v1-epoch3-training-fit.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-full-v1-epoch5-tuning.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-full-v1-epoch5-training-fit.json`.
- A matched 16-example diagnostic retained the generic pretrained T5 encoder between the new visual projection and decoder. After 20 mixed-objective passes, its eight-clip seen WER was 2.475 despite a 4.074 mean absolute teacher-forced logit change when videos were swapped. Twenty generation-only passes reduced WER to 0.646 with three exact transcripts; a second block reduced it to 0.135 with seven of eight exact. The remaining clip decoded as another learned training transcript. Its final swapped-video logit change was 3.080, so the route is strongly conditioned on visual input. At the same 60-pass diagnostic budget, this is better than the discarded direct route's WER 0.172 and six exact transcripts. It passes the memorization gate but does not establish held-out quality. A cleanly initialized full-inventory run is the next test; the 16-example weights will not initialize it. See `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-encoder16-v1-training-fit.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-encoder16-ce-v2-training-fit.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-t5small-encoder16-ce-v3-training-fit.json`.
- The expanded fresh-model inventory combines all available eligible public training rows in one index: 3,359 pre-cut How2Sign clips, 882 raw-video How2Sign clips and 313 FLEURS clips, for 4,554 examples. The schedule samples that combined inventory with source and signer balancing rather than treating the files as unrelated training sets. Its SHA-256 is `c8e3beb820435ac699a2040b7ee9c90bb7a0e8523f03bb3558579a8cfa7dfd6d`. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v2/training-inventory.jsonl`.
- The clean retained-encoder model uses aspect-preserving short-side 192 frames at 12.5 fps, MobileNetV3-Small, a new temporal encoder and the generic pretrained T5-Small encoder and decoder. It has 64,131,360 parameters, about 256 MB in FP32 or 128 MB in FP16 before packaging, compared with the 2.5 GB frozen baseline weights. Its best checkpoint reached How2Sign WER 0.9169 and FLEURS WER 0.9508. It beats the baseline on FLEURS but loses on How2Sign, so it is retained as efficiency evidence and not promoted. Downscaling to 192 uses 56.25% as many pixels per frame as short-side 256 at the same aspect ratio. The smaller parameter count comes from the architecture; downscaling reduces frame-processing cost and memory but does not itself remove model weights. See `checkpoints/fresh-lowres-v2-decoder-step1024-v2/run.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-v2-decoder-step1024-v2-tuning.json`.
- A matched Kinetics-400 S3D branch did not improve the fresh model. Its first repeated-exposure diagnostic had mean seen WER 2.645; generation-only continuation reduced that to about 0.63 but retained one runaway output and one wrong transcript. On open tuning data, a clean 512-example decoder run scored 1.1569 How2Sign and 0.9902 FLEURS. A 512-example alignment run scored 1.0674 and 1.0140, then worsened after 1,024 examples to 1.4160 and 1.1692. This branch is closed without confirmation scoring or miner promotion. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v2-s3d-retained16-ce-v3-training-fit.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-v2-s3d-clean-step512-v1-tuning.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-v2-s3d-alignment-step1024-v1-tuning.json`.
- The v3 raw-frame inventory combines 3,359 pre-cut How2Sign clips, 882 raw-video How2Sign clips, 313 FLEURS clips and the 1,024 ASL STEM Wiki clips, for 5,578 unique examples. Its SHA-256 is `c02aed7baca229e93d354c304ab0a294eeccaa8fb40d921ef506b6ef5f67d2e1`. A clean MobileNetV3-Small and T5-Small run completed all 6,204 scheduled exposures, including three exposures for each FLEURS row, at a 50% MPS duty cycle. It did not become competitive. The best unconstrained balanced checkpoint scored 1.140 How2Sign and 0.949 FLEURS; a fixed repetition-constrained frontier improved the best How2Sign point to 0.967 with FLEURS at 0.978, still behind the frozen baseline and the promotion threshold. A 512-example generation-only continuation did not recover the branch. A separate gentle visual-adaptation probe started from the prior best compact checkpoint and emphasized the new STEM clips; its best point scored 0.924 How2Sign and 1.067 FLEURS. Both v3 branches are retired without confirmation scoring or miner promotion. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v3/training-inventory.jsonl`, `checkpoints/fresh-lowres-v3-clean-decoder-epoch1/run.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-v3/clean-decoder-step-1536-constrained-tuning.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-v3/stem-visual-probe512-step-256-tuning.json`.
- The v4 fresh inventory adds 1,650 unused official How2Sign pre-cut clips to the aligned, raw-video and FLEURS sources, for 6,204 training rows. Decoder continuation regressed to 0.9220 How2Sign and 1.0223 FLEURS at its best new checkpoint. Encoder-only continuation reached 0.9225 and 1.0482 before worsening. A seeded `mild_v1` augmentation profile applies clip-consistent horizontal and vertical translations of up to 4% with reflect padding, contrast 0.9-1.1, brightness shifts up to 0.06 and mild Gaussian blur on 35% of clips. It does not flip signing or force square frames. The first augmented checkpoint preserved the prior FLEURS result at 0.9508 but worsened How2Sign to 1.1099; later checkpoints were worse. These results reject the current fresh architecture and augmentation recipe, not the general use of small aspect-preserving inputs. See `runs/fresh-lowres-v4/training-inventory.jsonl`, `runs/fresh-lowres-v4/decoder-step1024-v1-768-tuning.json`, `runs/fresh-lowres-v4/encoder-align-step1024-v1-512-tuning.json` and `runs/fresh-lowres-v4/encoder-align-aug-mild-step512-v1-128-tuning.json`.
- All 1,650 new How2Sign clips were resampled to 25 fps, extracted with the pinned Linux MediaPipe container and cached as stride-2 C5 streams. The completed stream index SHA-256 is `11c29a7afc81755eae3ded5f85c768ed2d3b6bee8bded86eb8c683e9496c987f`. Short projection-frozen continuations started from the confirmed corrective adapter and balanced all eight How2Sign signers with FLEURS replay. The strongest open-tuning point, the broader schedule at step 512 and learning rate `4e-6`, reduced How2Sign WER from 0.8341 to 0.8113 while leaving FLEURS at 0.8806. Only signers 2 and 5 improved, so it failed the frozen three-signer gate. A 134-clip internal holdout was then frozen from expansion clips unseen by that checkpoint. On this larger diagnostic, mean WER was flat at 0.92379 for the confirmed adapter and 0.92374 for the candidate; word-weighted WER changed from 0.90897 to 0.90589, with 9 clips improved, 116 tied and 9 regressed. Three signers improved and four worsened. The expansion candidate is rejected, and the confirmed C5 corrective stride-2 model remains the production candidate. See `runs/how2sign-precut-expansion-v1/stream-cache-stride2-index.jsonl`, `runs/c5-how2sign-precut-expansion-scale-v2/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-how2sign-precut-expansion-holdout-v1/decision.json`.
- A paired source-resolution check used the confirmed C5 stride-2 bundle on the 23 open tuning clips with identical Linux CPU MediaPipe landmarks. Native input scored 0.8215 mean WER on How2Sign and 0.8354 on FLEURS. Aspect-preserving short-side 256 worsened those means to 0.8750 and 0.8853; it regressed 7 of 16 How2Sign clips and 5 of 7 FLEURS clips. Short-side 384 recovered some quality, scoring 0.8496 and 0.8273, but still worsened How2Sign by 0.0281. Neither setting reduced active runtime because C5 still constructs 224-pixel DINO crops and pays for an extra full-frame resize. Both candidates are rejected. Source downscaling alone does not shrink C5; the compact-model path requires a smaller visual and language architecture. See `runs/c5-resolution-tuning-v1/decision-256.json` and `runs/c5-resolution-tuning-v1/decision-384.json`.
- Reducing only the DINO hand and face crops from 224 to 168 pixels was more promising. It cuts each visual stream from 256 to 144 patch tokens per frame. On open tuning, How2Sign changed from 0.8215 to 0.8294 mean WER while FLEURS improved from 0.8354 to 0.8157; paired active time fell 4.2%. A larger 134-clip internal How2Sign holdout then changed mean WER from 0.92379 to 0.93534 and word-weighted WER from 0.90897 to 0.91293. Pairwise outcomes were 28 improved, 82 tied and 24 regressed, but losses concentrated on signers 3 and 11. The unadapted 168-pixel runtime narrowly fails the quality gate and is not a production change. It is retained as a training direction: expose a new adapter to smaller and mildly jittered crops on training-only clips, then repeat the open and internal-holdout checks. See `runs/c5-resolution-tuning-v1/decision-visual-crop-168.json` and `runs/c5-resolution-holdout-v1/decision-visual-crop-168.json`.
- A training-only affine feature calibrator recovered the 168-pixel crop loss without changing the C5 translator. The selected fit used paired 168/224 DINO features from 169 How2Sign clips, balanced across all eight available signers and capped at 32 frames per visual stream. On open tuning, How2Sign improved from 0.8215 to 0.8101 mean WER and FLEURS from 0.8354 to 0.7756. DINO time fell 26%, while total paired active time fell 4.84%, just short of the predeclared 5% speed gate. On the 134-clip internal holdout, mean WER improved from 0.92379 to 0.91044 and word-weighted WER from 0.90897 to 0.90633, with 29 clips improved, 80 tied and 25 regressed; five of eight signer means improved. Adding deterministic clip-consistent horizontal and vertical shifts of up to 4% to the calibrator fit did not improve the open gate (How2Sign 0.8174, FLEURS 0.7756), so that variant was rejected. A one-shot cross-domain confirmation was then frozen before inference, selecting one clip from each of 60 OpenASL source videos. Against native 224-pixel crops, calibrated 168-pixel crops improved mean WER from 0.6320 to 0.6145 and word-weighted WER from 0.6205 to 0.6152; 15 clips improved, 35 tied and 10 regressed. Paired active time fell 6.7%. This passed every predeclared promotion gate. The confirmed standalone bundle is `artifacts/c5-corrective-stride2-crop168-confirmed`, identity SHA-256 `8c813666d4edadbf1e658b9edc747a2f6426950c90f44e712d48bec22b1cebf6`; its integrity and CPU loading were verified. It is now the production candidate but has not been installed in the miner. See `runs/c5-resolution-tuning-v1/decision-visual-crop-168-calibrated-v4-cap32.json`, `runs/c5-resolution-holdout-v1/decision-visual-crop-168-calibrated-v4-cap32.json`, `runs/c5-crop-openasl-confirmation-v1/decision.json` and `runs/c5-crop-calibration-v4-cap32/fit-report.json`.
- A controlled follow-up tested whether the repeated two-signer ceiling came from freezing C5's visual projection. Its 1,024-example schedule combined 512 How2Sign clips, 136 FLEURS clips, 256 ASL STEM clips and all 120 prepared OpenASL clips. Visual LoRA used learning rate `2e-6`; the three direct projection tensors used one-tenth that rate with a normalized anchor to their confirmed values. Maximum direct drift remained 0.15%. The best checkpoint, at step 1,024, improved cached-stream How2Sign WER from 0.8341 to 0.8219 and held FLEURS at 0.8806, but again only signers 2 and 5 improved. It missed the predeclared three-signer and equal-domain gates and is rejected. This rules out insufficiently gentle projection movement as the main cause of the narrow gains; substantially more independent signer/source coverage is a higher-value next step than another nearby C5 continuation. See `runs/c5-anchored-projection-v1/plan.json` and `runs/c5-anchored-projection-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- A compact text-correction pilot tested whether systematic language errors could be repaired without another visual update. The confirmed C5 adapter decoded a frozen, training-only mixture of 1,024 unique clips spanning eight prepared sources. Their mean WER was 0.7374, ranging from 0.4168-0.4398 on the two ASL STEM tranches to 0.8610-0.9485 on the How2Sign sources. A source-stratified split reserved 205 clips before fitting. T5-Small then trained on 819 hypothesis-to-reference pairs plus matched reference-to-itself identity examples for four epochs at a 50% MPS duty cycle. It did not generalize: epoch 1 worsened holdout WER by 0.1166; the best later epoch changed only three of 205 hypotheses and still worsened mean WER by 0.0040. One How2Sign raw example accounted for a 0.0348 source-level regression, while gains on the other sources were zero or negligible. The frozen -0.01 gate failed, so no open tuning or confirmation data was scored and the production candidate is unchanged. This closes language-only post-correction as a near-term path; the missing information is primarily visual, not a broadly learnable transcript rewrite. See `runs/c5-text-corrector-plan-v1/plan.json` and `checkpoints/c5-text-corrector-v1/decision.json`.

Some minority clips repeat up to 88 times, so overfitting remains a risk and this is not an equal-update comparison. Six new scheduler tests and 25 related tests pass. The proposal is archived under `runs/c5-stationary-mixture-proposal-v1/`; a compatible trainer and complete frozen scientific plan are still required before launch. No quality gain is claimed.

- The user requested an approach reassessment after a week without a broadly better trained model. The prepared coverage-order plan at `runs/c5-all-source-retention-coverage-v1/` remains unstarted. An input audit instead sampled 96 training clips (eight per How2Sign signer and per other prepared source) plus the 23 already-open tuning clips. It used pinned hand/face DINO assets to identify blank-crop embeddings. How2Sign face streams were frequently blank or repeated: all eight sampled signer-2 training clips had entirely blank face embeddings, while the How2Sign tuning signer groups had 91.6-98.3% exact adjacent face-feature repeats. The other sampled domains generally had more varying face features. These statistics measure the chosen clips; they do not establish semantic alignment or corpus-wide failure rates. A detector-only follow-up sampled three frames from one predetermined training clip per each of eight How2Sign signers. The unchanged full-frame detector found a face in 1/24 frames; a pose-guided head-region probe found faces in 24/24 using the same asset and pinned Linux CPU image. A viewed signer-2 frame visibly contained a face. This supports testing face recovery with fixed translation weights before further training; detection success is not translation-quality evidence. These references are absent from the prepared training corpus and prior validation selection. The corrected archive audit maps these candidates to 64 signer-specific recordings.
