- The upstream [SHuBERT paper](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1397.pdf) describes roughly 1,000 hours of representation pretraining and downstream fine-tuning on How2Sign and OpenASL. Access to those datasets alone is therefore not an established advantage over that lineage. The packaged checkpoint's exact training inventory is still undocumented: its retained `PROVENANCE.md` identifies upstream model revision `578a0233e770c8ce4dc75d859b91fdea7c34f5aa`, while the [publisher model card](https://huggingface.co/ShesterG/SHuBERT) contains no training details. Do not assert that locally prepared How2Sign/OpenASL clips are novel relative to its training data.
- The full-corpus retention comparison completed all 7,732 distinct clips and 1,933 updates on CPU, with visual gradients present and the direct projection unchanged. The recipe changed only by adding coefficient-1 KL against a frozen copy of the starting adapter, at temperature 2 on training-reference prefixes. The initial KL was exactly zero. At step 1,024, native cached-stream WER improved from 0.8341 to 0.8311 on How2Sign and from 0.8806 to 0.8392 on FLEURS, but only signers 2 and 5 improved; signer 1 regressed, signer 3 tied and signer 8 regressed slightly. No checkpoint passed all original gates. Final WER was 0.8504/0.8987, less degraded than the matched no-retention control's 0.9371/0.9451 but still worse than the starting model. The candidate is rejected, confirmations stayed sealed and the confirmed crop bundle remains selected. Loss-weight balancing did not ensure early exposure: the first 1,024 examples included nine signer-1 clips and 500 signer-8 clips. A coverage-balanced order is a separate hypothesis, not an established gain. See `runs/c5-all-source-retention-v1/plan.json`, `runs/c5-all-source-retention-v1/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-all-source-retention-v1/integrity.json`.
- Before the retention comparison, rechecking the historical 768-example schedule found 25 clips from protected internal How2Sign holdout recordings. That schedule was not reused. The two full-corpus experiments use the separately audited 7,732-clip manifest, which excludes protected source recordings as well as exact protected references. Historic 768-example outputs remain diagnostic records; they must not establish independence from the internal holdout. No new confirmation was decoded.
- A matched split-rate comparison did not solve retention. With visual LoRA at `1e-4` and decoder LoRA at `4e-6`, step 768 improved native cached FLEURS WER from 0.8806 to 0.7826 but worsened How2Sign to 0.8980. Reversing those rates reached How2Sign 0.8041 with four improved signers at step 128, but FLEURS worsened to 0.8868; later checkpoints regressed. Neither arm passed every original gate, and the joint frozen screen rejected both. See `runs/c5-openasl-split-rates-v1/decision.json`.
- The complete prepared continuous-sign corpus was then audited for a broader pass. Of 8,492 cached candidates, 7,732 remain after excluding 759 clips from protected How2Sign recordings and one exact protected reference. The eligible corpus contains 5,131 How2Sign clips, 313 FLEURS clips, 2,048 ASL-STEM clips and 240 OpenASL clips. A frozen full pass used all eligible clips once, balanced How2Sign signers within a 60% How2Sign objective mass, and assigned 20% to FLEURS, 15% to ASL-STEM and 5% to OpenASL. Visual/decoder peak rates were `1e-5`/`3e-5`, with 100 optimizer steps of warmup and cosine decay to 10% floors. All 1,933 updates completed with visual gradients present and the direct projection unchanged. No checkpoint passed: step 512 scored 0.8084 How2Sign and 0.8871 FLEURS with only two improved signers, while the final step regressed to 0.9371 and 0.9451. The confirmed calibrated crop bundle remains selected, and every confirmation set stayed sealed. This recipe supports testing an explicit retention objective against the starting model before further data-volume escalation. See `runs/c5-all-source-corpus-v1/audit.json`, `runs/c5-all-source-v1/plan.json` and `runs/c5-all-source-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- The corrected visual-gradient path now has a matched full-data result. Keeping the original 768-example mixture, seed, order, learning rate `4e-6`, checkpoints and quality gates unchanged improved the final native cached-stream How2Sign WER from the buggy control's 0.8152 to 0.8113; FLEURS remained 0.8806 and only two signers improved. All 192 updates retained gradients for all 16 visual adapter parameters. A separate training-only comparison repeated the same 24 new-source clips at `4e-6` and `1e-4` for 32 passes. The low-rate arm stayed near WER 0.600. The high-rate arm first passed its frozen fit gate at epoch 16, then reached WER 0.1009, 17 exact translations and 20 improvements at epoch 32, with 24 distinct outputs. Those diagnostic weights did not initialize a full-data run. A fresh 768-example replay at `1e-4` improved both native cached tuning domains at step 256 (How2Sign 0.8228, FLEURS 0.8481), but only two signers improved. Step 512 improved FLEURS to 0.8324 while worsening How2Sign to 0.8429; step 768 worsened How2Sign further to 0.9087. Every checkpoint failed at least one original gate, so the production candidate remains unchanged and all confirmations remain sealed. These results justify testing separate visual/decoder learning rates to balance adaptation and retention; they do not demonstrate held-out gains for the diagnostic fit. See `runs/c5-openasl-scale-fixed-v1/tuning-decision.json`, `checkpoints/c5-visual-trainability-v1/decision.json` and `runs/c5-openasl-scale-high-lr-v1/tuning-decision.json`.
- A real-model gradient audit on 2026-10-12 found that the visual LoRA path had been bypassed. Fairseq's fused SHuBERT attention reads `q_proj.weight` and `v_proj.weight` directly; the wrapper exposed only the frozen base weights there. All 16 installed visual LoRA parameters lacked gradients on the audited clip, and all eight visual B tensors were still zero in both the confirmed corrective adapter and the completed OpenASL-scale endpoint. The corrected weight property returns the base plus its low-rank update. On the same C5 input, zero-delta logits remain bit-identical; all eight B tensors then receive nonzero gradients, and one step changes the visual encoder output while preserving the direct projection. Previous claims that these experiments ruled out visual adaptation are therefore unsupported: those audited checkpoints learned through the decoder and, where enabled, the direct projection. Their recorded evaluation scores and the selected merged production bundle remain valid. A matched replay keeps the prior 768-example schedule, seed, learning rate, checkpoints and quality gates unchanged while enabling actual visual learning; gradient coverage is now checked after every update. See `runs/c5-visual-lora-gradient-fix-v1/audit.json`, `runs/c5-openasl-scale-fixed-v1/plan.json` and `tools/shubert_lora.py`.
- The completed 120-new-source OpenASL continuation failed every checkpoint's frozen open gate. Its final step 768 reduced matched native cached-stream How2Sign WER from 0.8341 to 0.8152 while leaving FLEURS at 0.8806; only signers 2 and 5 improved, signer 1 regressed, and signers 3 and 8 were unchanged. Its equal-domain gain was 0.00949 versus the required 0.01. The new 60-source confirmation stayed sealed, and the confirmed calibrated 168-pixel C5 bundle remains selected. A deterministic, training-only probe then selected one trained clip from each of 24 new sources: WER changed from 0.5964 to 0.6042, with zero improvements, 23 ties and one regression; only two hypotheses changed. This points to limited learning under the conservative adapter update, not a demonstrated failure of additional source diversity. A training-fit test with more adapter plasticity is the next informative experiment. See `runs/c5-openasl-scale-v1-bound/tuning-decision.json` and `runs/c5-openasl-scale-v1-bound/training-fit24-decision.json`.
- On 2026-10-12, user-authorized cleanup removed 272 obsolete fresh-model weight files totaling 43.69 GiB while hash-verifying all 153 retained files, including the strongest compact baseline and all experiment records. Physical free space did not rise immediately; two purgeable Time Machine snapshots remain on the APFS Data volume, which is consistent with deleted blocks being retained. Those snapshots were not changed. The deletion manifest and retained-file hashes are in `runs/checkpoint-cleanup-2026-10-12/manifest.json`. The user subsequently granted standing permission to delete old model files created by this training work when needed.
- Greg reported a DINO experiment in which retaining every second frame made inference 2.14 times faster and improved WER from about 0.80 to about 0.72. The dataset, sample count, split, exact frame rate and decoding setup were not supplied, so this is external evidence rather than a local timing measurement. A matched local check subsampled all four cached visual streams before the C5 temporal encoder. The frozen baseline's mean WER changed from 0.8891 to 0.8676 on How2Sign and from 0.9730 to 0.9511 on FLEURS-ASL. The strongest corrective adapter at step 384 reached 0.8341 and 0.8806 at stride 2, passing every open-tuning gate. Its subsequently frozen confirmation result remained positive on both domains: 0.9284 to 0.8935 on How2Sign and 1.0497 to 0.9578 on FLEURS-ASL. This workspace has not yet measured end-to-end stride-2 latency, so Greg's 2.14-times speedup remains external timing evidence. See `runs/temporal-stride-v1/corrective384-stride2-tuning-decision.json` and `runs/temporal-stride-v1/confirmation-decision.json`.
- Trupan [CLAW] reported a small C5-baseline resolution check on public FSboard-val and FLEURS-ASL devtest clips. With aspect ratio preserved and the short side reduced to 256 pixels, the reported FS score changed from 0.45 to 0.42 and the continuous score from 0.14 to 0.16. Forcing a 256 x 256 square, either by letterboxing or squashing, gave FS about 0.27-0.29 and continuous about 0.11-0.12. A 128-pixel short side gave continuous about 0.07. Trupan's preliminary reading is that preserving aspect ratio matters more than the exact pixel count, while 128 pixels is costly for continuous clips. These are external, small-sample results; the score definitions, sample counts, clip identities and preprocessing details were not supplied. The local paired check above did not reproduce a quality or latency benefit at short-side 256 or 384 on C5, so native source resolution remains the local runtime policy.
- The independent frozen baseline currently calls `VideoReader(path)` without a fixed output width or height, so it decodes source frames at their native dimensions. Its later hand and face crop extractors resize those crops to 224 x 224. A short-side-256 experiment here would need an explicit, separately evaluated preprocessing change; Trupan's C5 numbers do not measure this pipeline. See `artifacts/baseline-6fe8df59/runtime.py` and `artifacts/baseline-6fe8df59/umi_video_reader.py`.
- The official ChicagoFSWild metadata bundle is pinned locally at SHA-256 `d97fa42e67b9c78d9266dd9195c7ac0eaae90641e17fa02d510863f2c48f5f52`. Its two CSVs contain 7,234 and 55,231 rows, below the publisher page's 7,304 and 55,232 sequence counts. Their signer counts are 151 and 253, versus 160 and 260 on the page. Excluding training rows that share a held-out signer or a usable held-out source URL leaves 5,341 ChicagoFSWild and 48,187 ChicagoFSWild+ training rows. This is not yet a verified source-disjoint corpus: 993 and 525 training rows lack usable source URLs, ChicagoFSWild+ has seven signers shared between training and a held-out partition, and it has 511 repeated interval keys, including 123 with label disagreement. No frame archive has been downloaded. The publisher page does not state an explicit reuse license, so dataset and originating-video rights need a source-specific decision before frame ingestion or public-weight training. See `runs/chicagofswild-metadata-v1/audit.json` and `tools/audit_chicagofswild_metadata.py`.
- Resolution is now split into two independent ablation controls. `--source-short-side` performs downscale-only, aspect-preserving source resizing before MediaPipe; 256 is the primary setting, with 192 and 128 as stress tests. `--visual-crop-size` changes the square hand and face tensors presented to the frozen DINOv2 backbone. Because the pinned backbone uses 14-pixel patches, tested crop sizes must be multiples of 14. A 168-pixel crop has 144 patch tokens per stream frame instead of 256 at 224 pixels, a 43.75% token reduction and a 68.36% reduction in the patch-token attention matrix. A 112-pixel crop has 64 patch tokens, a 75% token reduction and a 93.75% reduction in that matrix. Lower resolution may improve generalization by suppressing signer, background and compression shortcuts, but it may also remove fingerspelling handshape detail. First measure the frozen baseline's quality and latency sensitivity on already-open tuning clips; then train low-resolution candidates with resolution augmentation or high-resolution-teacher consistency and compare them on source- and signer-held-out data. Do not use sealed confirmation or test data to choose the resolution. See `tools/resolution_policy.py`, `tools/benchmark_fleurs_dev.py` and `tools/benchmark_fsboard_baseline.py`.
- A one-clip cached-landmark diagnostic separated source resizing from DINO crop size. On the same public FSboard validation clip, native source plus 224-pixel crops gave CER 80.93 and native source plus 168-pixel crops gave CER 97.47. Short-side-256 source plus 168-pixel crops gave CER 26.87. All three outputs were unusable repetitive collapses, and the cached FSboard landmarks are not a faithful substitute for the baseline's own crop detections. These values are failure-mode diagnostics, not model-quality estimates. The 168-pixel crop reduced each DINO stream from about 3.8 seconds to about 1.9 seconds on this clip. Total runtime cannot be compared cleanly because the collapsed hypotheses had very different decode lengths. See `runs/resolution-ablation-v1/` and `tools/benchmark_fsboard_resolution.py`.
- A fresh low-resolution family is separate from C5 adaptation. Its clean-pretrained arm reads raw frames and may start from generic ImageNet MobileNetV3-Small or Kinetics-400 S3D weights and generic T5-family weights, but it cannot load the C5/SHuBERT checkpoint or any cached C5 encoder state. The temporal encoder and visual-to-language projection are new. Its strict scratch control uses the same topology with random visual and language weights. Run that control only after a clean-pretrained formulation passes the training-fit gate; failure to fit a small corpus with generic pretraining is already stronger evidence against spending compute on the random arm. A later matched resolution comparison should hold temporal sampling and training examples fixed, preserve aspect ratio, compare short-side 192 against 256, and use only source-disjoint How2Sign and signer-disjoint FLEURS tuning data for selection. Short-side 160 follows only if 192 stays competitive; 128 is deferred because the external pilot found a large continuous-sign penalty there. See `tools/fresh_lowres_model.py`, `tools/train_fresh_lowres.py` and `tools/probe_fresh_lowres_training_fit.py`.

## Small-input visual capacity hypothesis

The user proposes increasing model capacity while preserving the small pixel input. Input resolution and learned parameter count can be varied independently. The original custom visual encoder has 986,384 parameters, with a separate 99,328-parameter bridge and 41,625,344 pretrained language parameters. A capacity comparison should enlarge the scratch visual encoder first, retain the same aspect-preserving small inputs, complete frame sequence, pretrained language initialization, data/schedule and evaluation gates, and isolate capacity from resolution or augmentation changes. Approximate four- and sixteen-million-parameter targets were initially proposals; the executable presets and measured sizes are recorded below.

The evidence does not establish insufficient capacity as the failure cause. The successful fit used 256 passes over 24 training clips. The larger run was interrupted after 18,596 exposures and 5,264 distinct clips, before complete-corpus coverage, and has no matched full-corpus training-set generation score proving convergence. Its development failure can also reflect optimization, weak visual/language alignment or data coverage. Diagnose full-corpus training fit and video conditioning alongside held-out WER in any capacity comparison. The winner-training run remains the active priority; this records a future hypothesis without changing live pinned code or launching concurrent GPU work.

`tools/tiny_rgb_capacity.py` now constructs executable scratch-visual presets at 986,384, 3,663,056 and 16,928,592 parameters. Width/depth grow in visual spatial/temporal attention; the 79,184-parameter convolution stem, four spatial tokens per frame, input geometry, frame limits and language interface remain unchanged. This isolates that visual attention capacity path, not convolutional capacity or all possible small-image architectures. With a 512-wide language decoder, bridge counts are 99,328 / 197,632 / 263,168. The existing 41,625,344-parameter language component would give expected totals of 42,711,056 / 45,486,032 / 58,817,104. Language weights are supplied unchanged; none of these presets uses pretrained vision.

Six new CPU-only tests and three existing language-interface tests pass. Across all three presets, a small synthetic language fixture retains every supplied non-encoder weight, receives unchanged rectangular pixels/masks, produces pixel-conditioned logits and finite end-to-end gradients. These are structural tests with zero ASL examples, no optimizer updates and no saved weights. The new presets still require real language-asset qualification, CPU/Metal numerical and memory parity, training-only ASL fitting, complete training coverage and frozen held-out comparisons. They do not interrupt the winner job or establish a speed/generalization improvement. No pinned source was changed.

## Broader experimental search, October 14, 2026

The user broadened the methodological goal: beat the base model, with freedom to choose the approach. Architecture and training strategy may change in new experiments; public-model-track rights, protected-data exclusions, honest quality comparisons and the ban on miner changes still apply. Existing frozen experiments and their failed results remain unchanged.

The next prepared route gives small-input raw vision direct feature supervision before relying on captions alone. `tools/tiny_rgb_visual_teacher.py` adds disposable heads assigning the existing four spatial queries to face, left hand, right hand and body. They predict the three 384-dimensional DINO feature streams and 14-dimensional pose stream; masked cosine losses supervise valid nonzero visual targets, with SmoothL1 for pose. Targets are detached, absent hand features are excluded and padded frames are ignored. The student still consumes small raw pixels; no teacher network is needed at inference. A binding guard requires approved training roles plus exact source identity, video hash, sampled-frame count/index hash and duration-audit hash. It rejects temporal substitutions rather than interpolate them away. This guard requires authoritative parent/provenance receipts and does not replace those receipts.

Eleven new synthetic CPU tests verify loss/gradient flow into raw pixels and the convolution stem, detached teacher gradients, missing-hand/padding handling, shape/timeline/role rejection and preservation of existing visual weights/input geometry. Forty-seven focused tests pass. A read-only check matched all 24 pilot identities to protected training feature rows, verified the selected feature-cache/sidecar hashes and found matching 12.5-fps frame counts for every pair. All 24 raw sources are 25 fps. Matching lengths alone do not prove complete temporal provenance; the native source/index transform binding, immutable pilot plan and real-case numerical qualification remain required before learning with these targets. No teacher-supervised model has been trained, no new held-out prediction was made and no GPU job was added.

This is a new raw-pixel/DINO-stream auxiliary-supervision route, distinct from the earlier unsuccessful cached-stream/C5-hidden-state distillation. That earlier failure is retained and still limits confidence. [TinyViT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.10666) motivates transferring strong teacher knowledge into a small visual student; [GFSLT-VLP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.14768) motivates separating representation learning from translation fitting. Their ImageNet and non-ASL sign-language results are research precedents, not local quality evidence or a guaranteed ASL improvement.

Keep two alternative tracks available after the matched capacity control: winner-preserving residual/adaptor learning instead of another unrestricted full fine-tune, and training-only decoding/routing calibration with native complete-composite controls. Earlier old-C5 adapter, text-correction and decode-length failures remain relevant; do not repeat them under new names or optimize consumed confirmations. All candidate selection must ultimately compare against the actual C5 winner on source-disjoint quality, with complete fingerspelling/router behavior checked before promotion. The current capacity run remains immutable; the new objective is preparation for a subsequent, separately frozen experiment.

## Raw-pixel feature-teacher pilot launched

Native extraction receipts now bind all 24 training pilot clips and six additional real preflight clips to their actual feature-cache hashes, video bytes and sampled frame indices. The audit found that preflight clip `1bm6Ur4Z4BQ_12-8-rgb_front` used a 24-fps original in the earlier raw inventory, while its cached teacher used a 25-fps derivative. Equal sample counts did not establish alignment. The new teacher experiment explicitly selects the existing hash-verified 25-fps derivative, binds its original-to-derivative receipt and recomputes its canonical header/indices. All prior inventory rows, plans, pixels, results and original files remain unchanged. The 24 fitting clips require no such rebind. The source/reference/protected-role, cache hash, optional sidecar provenance, native frame count and deterministic even-frame indices all pass for the 30 pairs. This is declared extraction/source provenance, not a new human label-alignment guarantee.

`tools/train_tiny_rgb_visual_teacher.py` implements the separate teacher pilot. Its fresh medium vision exactly matches the seeded capacity control; disposable feature heads add 448,910 parameters for 4,111,966 total and 50 parameter tensors. There is no language/caption graph or update in this phase. Native-CPU activation equivalence, masked-padding gradients and CPU/Metal all-parameter gradients must pass on all six complete real cases before any update. Existing limits remain 0.005 relative gradients/norm, 0.0005 loss, 0.001 predicted-output difference and 0.0001 same-shape native equivalence. The compared outputs are predicted hand/face/pose features, not translator logits. Two future full-float AdamW moments are reserved during proof; failure records are written before guards. This does not qualify the earlier failing full language-decoder Metal path.

Once qualified, the pilot performs 64 passes / 1,536 visual-only AdamW updates at learning rate 0.0003, weight decay 0.01, clip 1, dropout 0.1 and no augmentation. Inputs are aspect-preserving 192-pixel-short-side RGB with complete 12.5-fps timelines. Feature checks at epochs 0/1/4/8/16/32/64 require at least 50% reduction from initial training feature loss and at least 0.05 extra clip-average loss for the wrong video. Correct/wrong controls use the same clip-average operation because source clips have unequal timelines; they do not fabricate frame alignment. This is a feature-learning feasibility gate, not a translation-quality gate, and it cannot authorize promotion or count as beating C5.

Sixty-four focused CPU/interface/arithmetic/data tests pass. ### Direct semantic-error reward: Jeff research

The owner requests a model-assigned semantic error percentage during training, with generic output receiving no content credit. The intended project is [firelex/jeff](https://github.com/firelex/jeff), clarified after an initial investigation of TypeSafe's separate hosted Jev service. Reviewed Jeff HEAD is `3720e7c66d73b79a6e90189f3d5b0d6ecf03ddc0`. Jeff supports local CPU, Apple MLX and llama.cpp inference. Its [quantized base model](https://huggingface.co/mstrasser/jeff-base-gguf) has a 672 MB Q4_K_M file and a 1.08 GB Q8_0 file; each adapter adds about 169 MB. These are download sizes, not measured process memory or inference times on this machine. Code declares MIT; the [base weights](https://huggingface.co/mstrasser/jeff-base) and [grounding adapter](https://huggingface.co/mstrasser/jeff-adapter-ground) declare Apache-2.0. Their cards disclose source-data and synthetic-teacher provenance; preserve those notices and review them before any redistribution. TypeSafe's hosted-service terms are for a different product.

The grounding adapter assesses source support, a plausible starting point for comparing a predicted English transcript with its human reference. No claim of ASL-specific scoring accuracy is established. Jeff returns decision distributions; its score API uses two to ten described levels. A normalized rubric score can represent estimated semantic error on a 0-100 scale, but raw confidence or a yes/no probability is not the fraction of meaning mistranslated. The v1.3 base is adapter-first; maintainers recommend v1.2 for unfamiliar zero-shot tasks. Their runtime requires Python 3.12+, Torch 2.14 and Transformers 5.17, so any pilot must be isolated from the live trainer's Python 3.10/Torch 2.2 environment.

Next experiment: a training-only calibration pilot containing exact meaning, paraphrases, generic filler, wrong entities/actions, polarity errors and invented details, with predeclared human labels and no development/test references as reward-training data. Test Jeff's error ordering and calibration before its scores drive sequence-level minimum-risk or policy-gradient updates. Keep human-caption supervision and the fixed C5 WER/generation gates. A text-only frozen judge has no differentiable path through its returned scalar; the sequence objective must use current ASL-model candidate log probabilities. No judge weights were downloaded, no scoring API was called, no semantic-reward optimizer update was performed and no live source was changed during this research.

The owner then clarified the desired rubric: **0 means nonsense/unrelated; 10 means exactly the same semantic meaning, including faithful paraphrases**. This supersedes the earlier error-percentage direction. Obvious non-semantic transcription noise may be tolerated, but meaningful differences must remain errors. The standalone, diagnostic-only comparison is explicitly **Jeff versus GPT-5.6 Terra at medium reasoning**, whose model ID and reasoning support were verified in [official OpenAI documentation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.6-terra).

Four synthetic rubric controls cover exact wording, a faithful paraphrase, generic filler and negation. Six additional pairs contain the owner's three already-open development references, each compared with the saved C5 output and our 2,048-update output. ### Existing visual weights with smaller native inputs (2026-10-17)

Read-only inspection of the pinned C5 bundle finds that `model/vendor/demo/dinov2_features.py` always applies `Resize((224,224))` before the network. Shrinking only crop output would therefore leave the network's input surface unchanged. Its pinned DINO `layers/patch_embed.py` asserts divisibility by its14-pixel patch side;64 is invalid for this backbone, while70/84/112 are valid. `models/vision_transformer.py` already interpolates positional embeddings for another valid patch grid. These are source constraints, not measured performance or quality.

Safetensors header-only inspection finds21,630,720 backbone parameters/176 tensors in each C5 hand and face file, excluding training heads. Shapes are patch projection `[384,3,14,14]`, position `[1,257,384]` and four register tokens. Total backbone parameters are43,261,440. The ordinary unmasked forward does not use its mask token, so a later learning candidate should freeze those768 values across both models; expected remaining scope is43,260,672 parameters, not yet verified on instantiated real models. Left/right share the hand backbone; original weights on disk and selected model are untouched.112px uses one quarter of224px input pixels; that arithmetic is not a measured speed claim. The [Uni-Sign repository](https://github.com/ZechengLi19/Uni-Sign) announces released How2Sign/OpenASL pose-only checkpoints. Its [publisher model card](https://huggingface.co/ZechengLi19/Uni-Sign) declares CC-BY-NC-4.0; the public file API reports `how2sign_pose_only_slt.pth` at1,186,925,007 bytes, LFS SHA-256 `1bfd5f3312f04e4736f0a52f4ef9535916e6de9676a2a0d00c708748683fb00d`. This is a candidate compact-input architecture, not verified better weights. The [paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.15187v3) uses69 selected pose points; its RGB variant processes112-pixel hand crops. Public source inspection finds CUDA-specific online inference and RTMPose133-point inputs. Existing MediaPipe coordinates are not a verified drop-in equivalent. No new weights, pose archives, dependencies or third-party source code were installed. Code-license and runtime-portability review remain separate from the model card's license.

Important independent warning: the [SHuBERT authors' Table1](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1397.pdf) reports that Uni-Sign's YouTube-ASL pretraining includes over72% of OpenASL test examples. This is a primary-paper warning, not our independent inventory audit. It prevents treating those published OpenASL numbers as clean cross-domain evidence without provenance checks; it does not establish overlap with any particular local confirmation selection.

The [SIGNET code](https://github.com/elsobhano/SIGNET) is MIT-licensed and supplies expert/gating training, but its inspected README does not supply a ready pretrained expert/translator download. Its [authors' project page](https://cogvis-cvssp.github.io/papers/signet/) reports How2Sign BLEU4=15.4 and BLEURT49.5, not a demonstrated improvement over our C5 model. [Sigma v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.21223v3) remains a research lead: its reproducibility statement promises code upon publication; no released checkpoint was verified. [SSVP-SLT](https://github.com/facebookresearch/ssvp_slt) supplies CC-BY-NC research code and preparation/training instructions; a deployable pretrained translator was not verified in the inspected instructions. These findings do not prove that weights exist nowhere, and none authorizes bulk data acquisition or a runtime upgrade.

A read-only join of the142 frozen native evaluation IDs in `runs/c5-winner-full-v2-early/plan.json` against official How2Sign metadata finds135 How2Sign clips and7 FLEURS clips. Of the135 How2Sign IDs,64 are in official `train` and71 in official `val`; the71 newly acquired development clips are all official `val`, from17 recordings. These cohorts are held out from our local training, but they must not all be called unseen to arbitrary pretrained weights. Any candidate trained on the full official How2Sign training split may already have seen64 controls. Do not silently remove them, redefine the fixed142 gate or call a result on them independent generalization. Audit a proposed model's actual training manifest and arrange an explicitly predeclared uncontaminated confirmation before promotion. C5's complete upstream training inventory is still unknown; this is not a newly established contamination finding for C5.

Metadata bindings for this join: official realigned train SHA-256 `67a8f4fb6dc066f38006b89ac84c27a4b05d7fa07d5e6952f997042724d7a847`, realigned val `1bf5d57ce90d61571776d06a179335d99648d8c94fc22b327d2abb328d26137c`, native evaluation plan `c3270ed287990d3fa838f546e30af66ee6095fd90e3e8051657ca716e40e56f1`. The join used exact `SENTENCE_NAME == example_id`, not references or new predictions. No evaluation was decoded, no scoring request was sent, and the current native Terra recipe, baseline and gates are unchanged.

The union contains468 ASL-STEM,31 How2Sign expansion and33 OpenASL scale clips. All eight reserved word-development signers and retained P0/P19 diagnostics remain excluded from STEM training. It binds all four original documents and checks exact planned/actual phrase roles. It performs no new media decode or transformed-overlap screen and establishes no model quality. The existing phrase caches contain64px crops; a future native112 phrase stage must decode original complete videos, reproduce native crop/pose timelines and qualify its model path. No optimizer recipe is selected or launched. Sentence session18449 retains its18:08:23Z observation; the frozen word-risk plan remains queued until that pipeline is terminal. C5 stays selected and the goal remains unmet.

## Hard boundaries

- Current owner direction: another chat owns miner and codex-lb maintenance. Do not inspect, restart, reconfigure or load-test either service, and do not query port8787 from this training work. Historical health-guarded extraction procedures below do not authorize service checks for the current runs. The approved v2 compute window expired at `2026-10-15T22:47:28Z`; current training uses50% GPU duty and2 CPU threads. Do not extend the temporary unthrottled window without new owner direction.

- The user requests commits and pushes for this work. Commit verified code, tests and research notes to `studio-competitive-training` and push that branch to `origin` at completed milestones. Keep datasets, transformed data, checkpoints, run logs and local artifacts out of Git; publish selected model weights through an explicit release workflow.
- The user grants standing permission to delete obsolete model weight files created by this training work when needed. Preserve the selected baseline and production candidates, dataset files, source assets, experiment plans, logs and evaluation results. Record exact removed weight paths and verify retained files after cleanup.
- The bounded OpenASL clip-only expansion uses an 80 GiB physical free-space floor and a separate 4 GiB total data budget, with 128 MiB headroom checked before each item. This replaces its earlier full-video-oriented 100 GiB operational guard; scientific selections, partitions, schedules and quality gates are unchanged. The full-video fetchers retain their original guards.
- Keep all training data, checkpoints, runs and local artifacts in the ignored `data/`, `checkpoints/`, `runs/` and `artifacts/` trees.
- Never train on service or evaluation material from any cohort, private holdout labels, miner assignments, or cohort result packages. Operational corpora under `/Users/sam/.local/share/umi/` are excluded from training.
- Keep source recording, session and signer groups in one split. Detect exact duplicates, re-encodes and overlapping intervals before splitting.
- Do not use test or devtest for model selection. Record every evaluation call.
- Preserve each source's original bytes, license, attribution and transform history. Apply the reported public-model-track counsel advice in `DATA_SOURCES.md`; it does not waive a source's access, attribution or redistribution conditions.
- Do not download multi-terabyte sources until storage and rights are settled. The Studio had about 309 GiB free when this workspace was created; FSboard alone is roughly 1.3 TiB.
- This machine runs an active UMI miner. Mining service has priority over training and data preprocessing. Before a resource-heavy job, check miner health and size the job to available CPU, memory, GPU and disk capacity. Monitor miner health during and after the job. Throttle or stop the job if miner requests slow or fail, or if resource pressure threatens serving; confirm the miner has recovered before resuming.
- The user temporarily authorizes unthrottled GPU training from 2026-10-13 00:48:21 UTC through 2026-10-14 00:48:21 UTC (ending October 13 at 8:48:21 p.m. America/New_York), then requires returning to 50%. Subsequent launches must use the approved private policy at `runs/c5-resource-policy-v1/gpu-budget.json` and an expiry-aware launcher such as `tools/launch_c5_timed_gpu_pilot.py`. It checks wall-clock and monotonic expiry after every GPU block and automatically restores equal active/idle pacing at expiry. Each launch records the resource override separately from its unchanged scientific recipe. CPU and memory limits stay bounded; miner services and certification material remain untouched. The already-running paired face-learning retry keeps its frozen 50% duty cycle.
- Outside that explicitly authorized window, GPU training must leave inference headroom and use no more than 50% of GPU capacity. The current MPS trainer enforces this as a conservative 50% duty cycle by idling for the same wall time used by each active step. This bounds average training occupancy but does not cap an individual Metal kernel's instantaneous utilization. Unthrottled duty permits active GPU time but does not promise measured 100% GPU utilization.
- If the user explicitly confirms that the miner is undergoing maintenance, bounded low-compute training may use the trainer's maintenance mode while the health endpoint is intentionally unavailable. Such a run must record `user_authorized_maintenance` instead of a miner revision and cannot be described as health-guarded. Return to stable-revision health checks when maintenance ends.
- On this host, the miner's local liveness endpoint is `http://127.0.0.1:8787/healthz`. Offline workers launched with the current shared health check abort if the endpoint fails, its model revision changes, or its response takes over one second. Check health around bounded, low-priority jobs. Local health does not establish external scoring or request success. Do not leave a heavy job unattended unless it checks miner health itself and exits on failure.
- Keep current extraction batches at no more than 128 new pre-cut How2Sign clips, 64 How2Sign sidecars or 32 FLEURS clips per process. A pre-cut worker reached about 27 GiB RSS after 107 clips on 2026-10-03. Longer sidecar and FLEURS passes were stopped during inference, their indexed files were verified, and short batches resumed. Recheck memory and miner health before increasing these limits.
- `tools/run_bounded_visual_extraction.py` advances these fixed public-data sources in successive short batches. For a writer already running, `--handoff-pid` and `--handoff-count` wait for that exact process to leave the source and require its complete batch count before starting another writer. The supervisor checks miner revision and latency every ten seconds, stops a worker above its per-source RSS cap or after 30 minutes without an indexed result, refuses to start below 80 GiB free disk, and stops on a failed batch. Its per-source logs are under `runs/visual-extraction-supervisor-v1/`.
- Do not stop, reconfigure or load-test the live miner.

Read `DATA_SOURCES.md` before selecting data. It records all sources currently known to the project, including noncommercial and permission-gated collections.
